To improve, focus on:

Detailed optimizer design:

Function optimize_log(a_init, end_time, rng, base_lr, topk_init, topk_end, beta_init, beta_end, patience, maybe max_steps):
- n = a_init.size
- Normalize a_init to sum 1: a = np.clip(a_init, tiny, None); a/=a.sum()
- x = np.log(a)
- m = zeros, v=zeros, t=0
- best_score = current_score(a), best_x/a copy
- cur_score = best_score
- no_imp=0; cur_pat=patience
- step count maybe.
- While time < end_time and maybe steps? Use while.
   At each iteration (batch of maybe 1 step):
      a = softmax(x, out=?) To avoid reallocation? We can maintain a separately and update? If using x, compute a = np.exp(x - x.max()); a/=sum. This O(n). Could be okay. But if x.max changes, okay.
      c = self_conv(a)
      M = max(c)
      score = 2*n*M (since sum=1)
      if score < best_score: update best, no_imp=0 else no_imp++
      If no_imp >= patience: perturb.
      Determine topk: interpolate from topk_init to topk_end over patience? Or over total. Use topk = min(c.size, int(...)). Maybe topk = 4 + 64*(1 - exp(-no_imp/...))? We can set based on progress ratio = min(1, (steps)/warmup). But without steps maybe use m? Simpler: topk = min(256, max(topk_init, int(topk_end * progress))) with progress = min(1, (time - start)/(duration))? But optimizer called within phase; we can pass current progress via phase. Alternatively topk grows with no_imp (when stuck, look at more peaks). Use topk = int(np.clip(topk_init + (topk_end-topk_init)*min(1, no_imp/patience), 1, 256)). So when improving fast, few; when stuck, more.
      beta: also grow with no_imp or phase. beta_start maybe 1.0, beta_end 50. beta = beta_init + (beta_end-beta_init)*min(1, no_imp/patience). But no_imp resets. Could use global phase progress passed as t01. We'll pass warm=0/1? Maybe optimize_run has fixed schedule from its own start/end: phase_progress = (now-start)/(end-time_start). Use beta=beta_start+(beta_end-beta_start)*progress, topk similarly. This anneals over phase regardless of no_imp. Also if no_imp, add more topk.
      Compute weights for top k:
         k_act = min(topk, c.size)
         part = np.argpartition(c, -k_act)[-k_act:] (if k_act==c.size all)
         vals = c[part]
         If using softmax: scale = max(vals)/beta? Actually beta high. temp = max(vals)/beta. weights = exp((vals - max)/temp); normalize. If beta<1, temp>max (smooth); if beta large, temp small.
         Also incorporate exact: if max top dominates? Previous exact_w. With log and topk, maybe always use softmax. For very high beta, one active enough.
      Compute G direct: zeros(n). For each index (idx, w): if w tiny skip. js=max(0, idx-n+1); je=min(idx, n-1). G[js:je+1] += w * a[js:je+1][::-1]. This is derivative of c_idx. Note c_idx =∑ a_i a_{idx-i}; derivative includes a_{idx-i}. For idx=0, js=0 je=0, G[0]+=w*a[0]. For idx=2n-2, G[n-1]+=w*a[n-1]. Good.
      g = G - np.dot(a,G) (tangent). Maybe scale by 2n? Not needed for Adam? Could scale to unit. Let norm = np.linalg.norm(g). If norm small, maybe break. We can normalize g to norm~1 to make lr absolute. Previous lr 0.08 on gradient raw? G magnitude roughly M? With a sum1, c max ~M ~0.5/n? Actually normalized sum1, c max ~ target/(2n) ~0.75/n ~1e-4 for n8192. G ~ M ~1e-4. Adam raw lr 0.08 maybe huge? But Adam step size ~lr (because m/sqrt(v) ~ sign). If g~1e-4, v~1e-8, sign ~1, step ~lr. So lr controls. Good. If we normalize g to unit, lr same. Better normalize to mean abs? Let scale = np.mean(np.abs(g)); if scale>0: g /= scale. Then lr is natural coordinate log change. Use lr initialized maybe 0.1. Adam handles.
      But if we normalize, magnitude of G not considered; some coords zero. Fine.
      Adam step:
         t +=1
         m = β1 m + (1-β1)g
         v = β2 v + (1-β2)g^2
         x_hat = m/(1-β1^t); v_hat=v/(1-β2^t)
         delta = lr * x_hat/(sqrt(v_hat)+eps)
         x -= delta
         Clip x to [-50,50] (maybe) to avoid extreme. Then a = softmax(x)? But if x clipped, many equal. Use x -= x.mean()? Not necessary; softmax subtracts max. To keep x stable, subtract mean each step? x = x - x.mean() maybe preserves a but prevents drift. However Adam momentum assumes x stable; subtracting mean okay. Do after step? If subtract mean, a unchanged. It can reduce drift. We'll set x -= np.mean(x) occasionally or each step. O(n) extra. Since softmax subtract max anyway, but large differences can overflow; clip range 60. We can do x = x - np.mean(x) each step to keep zero mean, then a=exp(x)/sum(exp). Sum exp not 1 until normalize. Use x max subtract.
      Actually if we subtract mean after clipping, a unchanged. Good.

      However computing a via softmax each iteration O(n) plus FFT O(n log n). Could we update a multiplicatively from g to avoid x? Maybe but x Adam is okay.

      Accept/backtracking: To avoid adaptive step destabilizing, we can do simple: compute score new; if not finite or new > best_score*1.5 + 1e-12, halve step (try again). But computing c for new already. We can implement adaptive lr: if score worsens by > factor, reduce lr *=0.7; if improved, maybe lr *=1.01 up to max. This is easier than backtracking. Keep current score. If score increases too much, revert x to previous best/last? We can store last_good_x. If bad, revert and reduce lr. However nonmonotonic can escape; but for reliability, use if new_score > cur_score*1.2 + 1e-10 and t small? Maybe just track best and if no improvement restart; allow cur to wand. But to avoid divergence, if not finite revert.

      Since objective with x softmax and soft gradient should be smooth; Adam with moderate lr likely stable. Use lr schedule: start maybe 0.05, decay with progress *0.9995^steps? We can pass base_lr and phase. Simpler: lr = base_lr * (0.2 + 0.8*(1-progress))? But we have phases. In optimize_log, we can do lr = init_lr * (0.3 + 0.7*(1-progress)) plus adaptive halving on no_imp? We'll pass init_lr.

   Perturbation on stagnation:
      x = np.log(best_a) (where best_a stored) + rng.normal(0, sigma, n)*sigma. sigma start maybe 0.3 decays? Clip. Reset m,v,t, lr=base_lr*0.5, no_imp=0, patience half. This escapes.
   Need best_a stored as a array.

But note: If we normalize a to sum1, final sequence scale arbitrary. The evaluate_sequence objective scale invariant, but it caps at 1000 and sums. We can output a (sum1) maybe values small (1/n). The evaluator computes with floats, sum~1, conv max ~1/n, score ok. It clips negatives none. It also if sum_a <0.01 returns inf; sum=1 okay. So output normalized values fine. Function make_inits(n, rng, parent=None): returns list (name, a normalized sum1).

- If n==parent and parent exists include parent.
- ones: np.ones(n); a = ones/n.
- binomial one-sided: alpha_i = coeff of (1-z)^(-1/2): can generate recursively to avoid gamma: a0=1; a_{i}=a_{i-1}*(2i+? formula). Coefficient α_i = (2i choose i)/4^i. O(n) Python loop; n=16k fine but could vectorize with cumprod: ratios = (2*arange(1,n)-1)/(2*arange(1,n)); alpha = cumprod([1, ratios]). Use np.cumprod.
- reversed binomial.
- sum binomial + reversed maybe but normalized.
- power one-sided: a=(1+arange)^-0.5? Builtin. Binomial is similar but starts high and decays slowly; use both.
- Both edges mild: a = (1+arange)^-0.3 + (1+arange[::-1])^-0.3? Or 1/np.sqrt(arange+1) + small rev? Maybe include.
- sin powers: x=(arange+0.5)/n; a = np.sin(np.pi*x)**p for p in [-0.25,0,0.25]? p negative edges high but finite; normalize. p=0 uniform.
- cosine modulations: a=1+0.5*cos(2πx), 1+0.3*cos(4πx), clipped positive.
- gapped: ones with odds 0.5.
- ramp: 1+x.
- random smooth: generate white normal, lowpass by FFT multiply exp(- (freq)^2 * sigma), take abs, add 0.1, normalize. Need top-level function? Could.

Direct gradient vectorized optimized:
```
def grad_direct(a, idx, w, n):
    g = np.zeros(n)
    for ki, wi in zip(idx, w):
        if wi <= 1e-12: continue
        ki=int(ki)
        lo = max(0, ki - n + 1)
        hi = min(ki, n - 1)
        g[lo:hi+1] += wi * a[lo:hi+1][::-1]
    return g
```
This loops Python over k. For topk=128 and n=16k, slice assignment 128; okay. Could be a bottleneck. We can improve by using FFT for dense weights when topk > threshold (e.g., 64):
```

Adam implementation with top-level function:
```
def adam_update(x, g, m, v, t, lr, eps=1e-8, beta1=0.9, beta2=0.999):
    t1 = beta1 ** t
    t2 = beta2 ** t
    m = beta1*m + (1-beta1)*g
    v = beta2*v + (1-beta2)*g*g
    x_hat = m/(1-t1)
    v_hat = v/(1-t2)
    x -= lr * x_hat/(np.sqrt(v_hat)+eps)
    return
```
Computing beta1**t each step may be slow; maintain bias terms? Use t integer; `b1_t = b1 * b1_t_prev`; `b2_t = b2 * b2_t_prev`. We'll keep variables in loop.

But if g normalized, scale maybe dominated by big a. That's okay. Need normalize gx to mean abs? If we normalize gx, small a coordinates may get relatively larger steps due to normalization? But still sign. Let's use exact gradient: `gx = a * (G - np.dot(a,G))`. Scale: `gscale = np.mean(np.abs(gx))`. This gradient magnitude ~a*h; for uniform g~1/n, after scale unit. Good.

But if a has zeros, gx zero, cannot increase zero variables. Since we keep min positive, okay. Multiplicative stability.

Thus optimizer:
- state a positive sum1.
- compute h = G - dot(a,G).
- gx = a * h.
- normalize gx.
- Adam gives Δx.
- a *= exp(Δx); normalize.
This is Adam on x with exact gradient. Good.

For perturbation: a = best_a * exp(noise*sigma); normalize.

Learning rates: Since gx normalized, Δx ~ lr. Use lr 0.1 exploration, 0.03 polish. Good.

For random smooth lowpass function without lambda:
```
def smooth_random(n, rng):
    z = rng.standard_normal(n)
    F = np.fft.rfft(z, 2*n)
    k = np.fft.rfftfreq(2*n)*2*n
    F *= np.exp(-0.5*(k/(n*0.5))**2)
    y = np.fft.irfft(F, 2*n)[:n]
    return np.abs(y) + 0.1
```

Gradient direct function top-level:
```
def grad_direct(a, idx, w, n):
    g=np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    for idx_i in range(len(idx)):
        wi = w[idx_i]
        if wi <= 1e-14: continue
        k=int(idx[idx_i])
        lo = k - n + 1
        if lo < 0: lo=0
        hi = k
        if hi >= n: hi=n-1
        if lo <= hi:
            g[lo:hi+1] += wi * a[lo:hi+1][::-1]
    return g
```
Maybe speed: use local variables.

Gradient FFT:
```
def grad_fft(a, idx, w, A, n, nfft):
    N=2*n-1
    wrev = np.zeros(nfft, dtype=np.float64)
    # Need w length N; idx may up to 2n-2=N-1.
    wtmp = np.zeros(N, dtype=np.float64)
    wtmp[idx] = w
    wrev[:N] = wtmp[::-1]
    W = np.fft.rfft(wrev, nfft)
    h = np.fft.irfft(A * W, nfft)
    G = h[N-1:n-2:-1]
    # length should n
    if G.size != n: ...
    np.maximum(G,0,out=G)
    return G
```

G = h[start:stop:-1]

Softmax weights compute topk indices:
```
def topk_weights(c, k, beta, rng=None):
    L = c.size
    if k >= L:
       idx = np.arange(L)
       vals = c
    else:
       part = np.argpartition(c, -k)[-k:]
       vals = c[part]
    maxv = float(np.max(vals))
    if maxv <= 0:
       w = np.zeros_like(vals); w[0]=1; return part,w
    temp = maxv / max(beta,1e-6)
    # If temp too small, underflow; set temp=max(temp, maxv*1e-6)
    z = np.exp((vals - maxv)/temp)
    s = z.sum()
    if s <=0 or not finite:
       w = zeros; w[argmax]=1
    else w=z/s
    # Sort? Not necessary.
    return part, w
```
If beta <1, temp > maxv; weights smooth. If beta very large, temp small, maybe only top. Good.
Could include a small floor: weights = max(weights, 1e-8/ k)? Not necessary.

Topk schedule: In optimizer, we can have `topk_min`, `topk_max`, `beta_min`, `beta_max`. At iteration, progress p = min(1, (now - t_start)/(t_end - t_start)) maybe. topk=round(topk_min + (topk_max-topk_min)*p). If no_imp > patience/2, boost topk min(topk+16,max). Also boost beta.
But if p near 1, topk high. For exploration, topk_max maybe 16/32; for polish topk_max 128 and beta high.
We need pass scheduler type.

Optimize_log signature:
```
def optimize_log(a0, end_time, rng, init_lr, topk_min, topk_max, beta_min, beta_max, patience, label, max_iter=None):
```
Inside:
- n, a normalized copy.
- nfft.
- compute c,A,score.
- best_score=score, best_a=a.copy()
- stable_a=a.copy(), stable_score=score, current_score=score
- m,v zeros, t=0, b1t=1, b2t=1, lr=init_lr
- no_imp=0, cur_pat=patience, step=0
- t_start=time.monotonic()
- while time<end_time:
    step+=1
    # progress
    total_dur=end_time - t_start
    progress = min(1.0, max(0.0, (time.monotonic()-t_start)/total_dur))
    topk = int(topk_min + (topk_max-topk_min)*progress)
    topk = max(3, min(topk, c.size, 128? maybe max param))
    if no_imp > cur_pat//2:
       topk = min(c.size, topk + 8)
    beta = beta_min + (beta_max-beta_min)*progress
    if no_imp > cur_pat//2:
       beta = min(100.0, beta*1.5)
    # if score no improve for many, maybe reduce lr? lr *= 0.9 each no_imp? Could.
    if no_imp > 0:
       lr *= 0.999? maybe too slow. Use step decay: lr = init_lr * (0.3 + 0.7*(1-progress))? We'll set in phases separately; don't adapt much except reject.
    # compute weights and G
    idx,w = topk_weights(...)
    use_fft = topk > 24
    if use_fft: G = grad_fft_sparse(a, idx, w, A, n, nfft, wrev_work)
    else G=grad_direct(...)
    h = G - float(np.dot(a,G))
    gx = a * h
    scale = float(np.mean(np.abs(gx)))
    if scale <= 1e-30: # at optimum? break or perturb
        no_imp += cur_pat # trigger restart
    else:
       gs = gx / scale
       # Adam update
       t +=1; b1t = 0.9*b1t; b2t=0.999*b2t
       m[:] = 0.9*m +0.1*gs
       v[:] = 0.999*v +0.001*gs*gs
       step_arr = (m/(1-b1t))/(np.sqrt(v/(1-b2t))+1e-8)
       # could scale step by lr
       delta = -lr * step_arr
       np.clip(delta, -2.0, 2.0, out=delta)
       # update a
       np.exp(delta, out=tmp)
       a *= tmp
       s = a.sum()
       if not finite(s) or s <= 1e-300:
           a[:] = best_a
           lr *=0.5
       else:
           a /= s
           # clip minima? np.maximum(a, 1e-300, out=a); a/=a.sum()
    # recompute c,A,score
    A = np.fft.rfft(a,nfft)
    c = np.fft.irfft(A*A,nfft)[:N]
    score = 2*n*c.max()
    if not finite: revert etc.
    if score < best_score:
       best_score=score; best_a=a.copy(); no_imp=0
       # print maybe every 50 improvements
    else:
       no_imp +=1
       if no_imp >= cur_pat:
           # perturbation
           base = best_a
           sigma = 0.25 * (1.0 - 0.5*progress)  # maybe smaller late
           noise = rng.normal(0.0, sigma, n)
           # perhaps scale noise by log best: np.exp(noise)
           a = base * np.exp(noise)
           a = np.clip(a, 1e-300, None)
           s=a.sum(); a/=s
           m.fill(0); v.fill(0); t=0; b1t=1; b2t=1
           lr = min(init_lr, lr*0.7) if step large? maybe `lr = init_lr * 0.5`
           no_imp=0
           cur_pat = max(50, cur_pat//2)
    # optional adapt lr if score current > best by factor? no
# return best_score, best_a
```
Need `tmp` preallocated length n.
`wrev_work` preallocated length nfft for grad_fft. But `grad_fft_sparse` needs to zero it; could use global? We can allocate inside function top-level:
```
def grad_fft_sparse(a, idx, w, A, n, nfft, wrev):
    wrev.fill(0.0)
    N=2*n-1
    for ii in range(len(idx)):
       wrev[N-1-int(idx[ii])] = float(w[ii])
    W = np.fft.rfft(wrev, nfft)
    h = np.fft.irfft(A * W, nfft)
    G = h[N-1:n-2:-1]
    G = np.maximum(G, 0.0)
    return G.copy()
```
If h slice returns view of h; h local, need copy? `G = np.array(h[N-1:n-2:-1], copy=True)`. Then max in place.

    update
```

Pseudo code detailed:
```
def optimize_log(a0, end_time, rng, init_lr, topk_min, topk_max, beta_min, beta_max, patience, label):
    n = int(a0.size)
    a = np.asarray(a0, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    a = np.clip(a, 1e-300, None)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s <= 0: a = np.ones(n)/n
    a /= s
    N = 2*n - 1
    nfft = 1 << (N - 1).bit_length()
    # prealloc
    m = np.zeros(n)
    v = np.zeros(n)
    tmp = np.empty(n)
    wrev = np.zeros(nfft)
    # first fft
    A = np.fft.rfft(a, nfft)
    c = np.fft.irfft(A*A, nfft)[:N]
    M0 = float(np.max(c))
    score = 2.0*n*M0
    best_score = score
    best_a = a.copy()
    stable_a = a
    current_score = score
    b1=0.9; b2=0.999
    b1_t=1.0; b2_t=1.0
    t=0
    lr=init_lr
    max_lr=init_lr*1.5
    no=0
    cur_pat=patience
    t_start=time.monotonic()
    # ensure end_time finite
    if not np.isfinite(score) or score > 100: maybe still.
    last_print = -1
    improve_count=0
    while time.monotonic() < end_time:
       if time.monotonic() > end_time: break
       dur = end_time - t_start
       if dur <=0: break
       progress = (time.monotonic() - t_start)/dur
       if progress <0: progress=0
       if progress >1: progress=1
       topk = int(topk_min + (topk_max-topk_min)*progress)
       if topk < 3: topk=3
       if topk > N: topk=N
       if no > cur_pat//2:
           topk = min(N, topk + 16)
       beta = beta_min + (beta_max - beta_min)*progress
       if no > cur_pat//2:
           beta *= 1.5
       k=topk
       # topk weights
       if k >= N:
          idx = np.arange(N)
          vals = c
       else:
          part = np.argpartition(c, -k)[-k:]
          vals = c[part]
       vmax = float(np.max(vals))
       if vmax <= 0: idx=np.array([0],int); vals=np.array([0.0]); w=np.array([1.0])
       else:
          temp = vmax / max(beta,1.0)
          if temp < vmax*1e-8: temp = vmax*1e-8
          z = np.exp((vals - vmax)/temp)
          zsum = float(np.sum(z))
          if not finite or zsum<=0:
              w = np.zeros(k); w[np.argmax(vals)]=1
          else:
              w = z / zsum
       if k > 24:
          G = grad_fft_sparse(a, part, w, A, n, nfft, wrev)
       else:
          G = grad_direct(a, part, w, n)
       meanG = float(np.dot(a, G))
       h = G - meanG
       gx = a * h
       scale = float(np.mean(np.abs(gx)))
       if not finite(scale) or scale < 1e-30:
          no += max(1, cur_pat//4)
       else:
          stepg = gx / scale
          t += 1
          b1_t *= b1
          b2_t *= b2
          m = b1*m +0.1*stepg  # but m variable? Need in-place to avoid allocation:
```

          delta = -lr * step_arr
          np.clip(delta, -2.0, 2.0, out=delta)

def normalize_positive(a):
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s <= 1e-300:
        return None
    a = a / s
    a = np.maximum(a, 1e-300)
    a /= a.sum()
    return a
```

Resampling without closure:
```
def resample_sequence(parent, n):
    m = parent.size
    x_old = np.linspace(0.0,1.0,m)
    x_new = np.linspace(0.0,1.0,n)
    vals = np.interp(x_new, x_old, parent)
    return vals
```
If parent small? okay.

Smooth random top-level:
```
def smooth_random(n, rng):
    z = rng.standard_normal(n)
    L = 1 << (2*n - 1).bit_length()
    F = np.fft.rfft(z, L)
    k = np.fft.rfftfreq(L) * L
    cutoff = n / 4.0
    F *= np.exp(-0.5 * (k / cutoff)**2)
    y = np.fft.irfft(F, L)[:n]
    return np.abs(y) + 0.1
```
This uses FFT; okay.

Let's design `gradient_logsumexp(c, a, A, n, nfft, wrev, beta)`:
```
def gradient_logsumexp(c, A, n, nfft, wrev, beta):
    N=2*n-1
    M = float(np.max(c))
    if M <= 0:
       G = np.zeros(n); G[0]=1? return
    temp = M / max(beta, 1e-6)
    # If temp very small, maybe use top exact? Use threshold to avoid underflow: if beta > 50, exact active? But underflow OK.
    z = np.exp((c - M) / temp)
    s = float(np.sum(z))
    if not finite or s<=0:
        z.fill(0); z[int(np.argmax(c))]=1.0
    else:
        z /= s
    wrev.fill(0.0)
    wrev[:N] = z[::-1]
    W = np.fft.rfft(wrev, nfft)
    h = np.fft.irfft(A * W, nfft)
    G = h[N-1:n-2:-1]
    G = np.maximum(G,0)
    return G
```
But we need output into preallocated G to avoid allocation. We can have `z_work` length N, `G_work`.
```

```
This modifies z_work only.
Slicing G returns reversed array; need copy into G_work.
```

np.maximum(G_work, 0.0, out=G_work)
```

Now, implement `fft_logsumexp_grad_into(c, A, z_work, wrev_work, G_work, n, nfft, beta)`:
```
def fft_logsumexp_grad_into(c, A, z_work, wrev_work, G_work, n, nfft, beta):
    N = 2*n - 1
    M = float(np.max(c))
    if M <= 1e-300:
        G_work.fill(0.0)
        G_work[0] = 1.0
        return G_work
    temp = M / max(beta, 1e-6)
    # To improve numerical when beta large, subtract M and maybe threshold: if (c-M)/temp < -745, exp underflows.
    np.subtract(c, M, out=z_work)
    z_work /= temp
    np.exp(z_work, out=z_work)
    s = float(np.sum(z_work))
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        z_work.fill(0.0)
        z_work[int(np.argmax(c))] = 1.0
    else:
        z_work /= s
    wrev_work.fill(0.0)
    wrev_work[:N] = z_work[::-1]
    W = np.fft.rfft(wrev_work, nfft)
    h = np.fft.irfft(A * W, nfft)
    if n == 1:
        G_work[0] = h[0]
    else:
        start = N - 1
        stop = n - 2
        np.copyto(G_work, h[start:stop:-1])
    np.maximum(G_work, 0.0, out=G_work)
    return G_work
```
Need `z_work` length N. `wrev_work` length nfft.

I think scipy does; numpy? ufunc? Let's check: np.fft.irfft(x, n=None, axis=-1, norm=None, out=None) in recent numpy has `out`. Could use to avoid allocation: `h_work = np.empty(nfft); np.fft.irfft(A*W, nfft, out=h_work)`. But `A*W` still alloc complex. Could prealloc `prod = np.empty((nfft//2+1), complex); np.multiply(A,W,out=prod); np.fft.irfft(prod,nfft,out=h_work)`. This saves allocations. Let's do for performance.
Similarly for c: prealloc `c_work`, `A_work` complex? rfft `out` exists maybe. We can prealloc A_work complex length nfft//2+1, c_work float N, h_work float nfft.
But `np.fft.rfft` out parameter? In numpy, `rfft` has `out` in recent? I think yes? To be safe, not rely? Modern likely. We can use without out for simplicity. Simpler code accepted. But performance maybe okay.

Dense power weights function:
```
def power_grad_into(c, A, r_work, wrev, G, n, nfft, p):
   M = max(c)
   if M <= tiny: G fill 0; G[0]=1; return
   np.divide(c, M, out=r_work)
   np.power(r_work, p, out=r_work)
   s=sum(r_work)
   if s <=0: underflow? max should be 1^p=1, so s≥1 unless M inf. But due to p huge and M? 1 stays 1. So s>=1.
   r_work /= s
   wrev.fill(0); wrev[:N]=r_work[::-1]
   W=rfft(wrev); h=irfft(A*W); copy G.
```
This is robust. We can use p as float. For p large, r^p of small values 0; max 1. Good.
No underflow of sum.

This is perhaps better than logsumexp. We'll adopt.

Let's write `fft_power_gradient` carefully:
```
def fft_power_gradient(c, A, n, nfft, p, r_work, wrev_work, G_work):
    N = 2 * n - 1
    M = float(np.max(c))
    if M <= 1e-300:
        G_work.fill(0.0)
        G_work[0] = 1.0
        return G_work
    np.divide(c, M, out=r_work)
    np.clip(r_work, 0.0, 1.0, out=r_work)
    np.power(r_work, p, out=r_work)
    s = float(np.sum(r_work))
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        r_work.fill(0.0)
        r_work[int(np.argmax(c))] = 1.0
        s = 1.0
    else:
        r_work /= s
    wrev_work.fill(0.0)
    # assign reverse
    wrev_work[:N] = r_work[::-1]
    W = np.fft.rfft(wrev_work, nfft)
    h = np.fft.irfft(A * W, nfft)
    if n == 1:
        G_work[0] = h[0]
    else:
        start = N - 1
        stop = n - 2
        np.copyto(G_work, h[start:stop:-1])
    np.maximum(G_work, 0.0, out=G_work)
    return G_work
```
For p=0? We might use start 0.2, not 0. If p=0, `r^0` -> 1 (including zeros), sum N; okay but np.power(0,0)=1? maybe. We'll avoid p=0.

In optimize, c is c_work; but gradient function takes c, A, etc.

Potential issue: `wrev_work[:N] = r_work[::-1]` when N = nfft? For n power of two? N=2n-1, nfft next power of two >=N, so N <= nfft. Good. `wrev_work.fill(0)` zeros all nfft; then assign N reversed. Good.

       # recompute score from a? We have c from previous update; but after step update need recompute. Let's compute at top using c_work.

Compute initial score for each. We can use `initial_score(a)`: uses FFT. To avoid many nfft computations? fine.
```
def initial_score(a):
    a = normalize_positive(a)
    if a is None: return np.inf, None
    n = a.size; N=2*n-1; nfft=1 << (N-1).bit_length()
    A = np.fft.rfft(a, nfft)
    c = np.fft.irfft(A*A, nfft)[:N]
    c = np.maximum(c,0)
    return 2*n*float(np.max(c)), a
```
This returns normalized a.

Let's draft code mentally.

```
import numpy as np
import time

def normalize_positive(arr):
    try:
        a = np.asarray(arr, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    except Exception:
        return None
    if a.size == 0:
        return None
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300:
        return None
    a = a / s
    a = np.maximum(a, 1e-300)
    a = a / a.sum()
    return a
```
The evaluator clips values to [0,1000]. We can normalize. If parent values huge, clip.

`resample_sequence`:
```
def resample_sequence(parent, n):
   p = np.asarray(parent, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
   m = p.size
   if m == 0: return None
   x_old = np.linspace(0.0,1.0,m)
   x_new = np.linspace(0.0,1.0,n)
   vals = np.interp(x_new, x_old, p)
   return np.maximum(vals, 0.0)
```

`smooth_random`:
```
def smooth_random(n, rng):
   z = rng.standard_normal(n)
   L = 1 << (2*n - 1).bit_length()
   F = np.fft.rfft(z, L)
   k = np.fft.rfftfreq(L) * L
   cutoff = max(1.0, n / 4.0)
   F *= np.exp(-0.5 * (k / cutoff)**2)
   y = np.fft.irfft(F, L)[:n]
   return np.abs(y) + 0.1
```
If n=1, L. okay.

`push_init`:
```
def push_init(inits, name, arr):
    a = normalize_positive(arr)
    if a is not None:
        inits.append((name, a))
```

`make_inits`:
```
def make_inits(n, rng, parent):
    inits=[]
    i = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64)
    push_init(inits, "ones", np.ones(n))
    # binomial
    if n > 0:
       alpha = np.empty(n)
       alpha[0]=1.0
       if n>1:
          j = np.arange(1,n, dtype=np.float64)
          ratios = (2.0*j - 1.0)/(2.0*j)
          alpha[1:] = np.cumprod(ratios)
       push_init(inits, "binom", alpha)
       push_init(inits, "binom_rev", alpha[::-1])
       push_init(inits, "binom_mix25", alpha + 0.25*alpha[::-1])
       push_init(inits, "binom_mix50", 0.75*alpha + 0.25*alpha[::-1])? Already.
    for pval in (0.2,0.3,0.4,0.5):
       push_init(inits, f"pow{pval}", (i+1.0)**(-pval))
    # both
    push_init(inits, "pow_5_2", (i+1.0)**(-0.5) + (n-i+1.0)**(-0.2))
    push_init(inits, "pow_3_3", (i+1.0)**(-0.3) + (n-i+1.0)**(-0.3))
    push_init(inits, "pow_5_0", (i+1.0)**(-0.5) + 0.1*(n-i+1.0)**(-0.1)? maybe)
```

```

`select_candidates` no lambda:
```
def select_candidates(cands, max_count):
    cands_sorted = sorted(cands)  # sorts by score, id, n, name, a? id unique so array not compared if id different; if score and id? id unique.
    selected=[]; sel_id=set(); sel_n=set()
    limit_n = min(max_count, len(cands_sorted))
    for cand in cands_sorted:
        if cand[2] not in sel_n:
            selected.append(cand); sel_id.add(cand[1]); sel_n.add(cand[2])
            if len(selected) >= limit_n: break
    for cand in cands_sorted:
        if cand[1] not in sel_id:
            selected.append(cand); sel_id.add(cand[1])
            if len(selected) >= max_count: break
    return selected
```

`optimize_log` prealloc work arrays. Let's write in detail.

```
def optimize_log(a0, end_time, rng, init_lr, p_start, p_end, patience, label):
    a = normalize_positive(a0)
    if a is None:
        n = int(np.asarray(a0).size) if a0 is not None else 256
        a = np.ones(n) / n
    n = int(a.size)
    N = 2*n - 1
    nfft = 1 << (N - 1).bit_length()
    m = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    v = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    r_work = np.empty(N, dtype=np.float64)
    wrev_work = np.empty(nfft, dtype=np.float64)
    G_work = np.empty(n, dtype=np.float64)
    h_work = np.empty(n, dtype=np.float64)
    stepg = np.empty(n, dtype=np.float64)
    q_work = np.empty(n, dtype=np.float64)
    sqrtv = np.empty(n, dtype=np.float64)
    delta = np.empty(n, dtype=np.float64)
    tmp = np.empty(n, dtype=np.float64)
    noise_work = np.empty(n, dtype=np.float64)
    c_work = np.empty(N, dtype=np.float64)
    ...
```
First compute:
```
A = np.fft.rfft(a, nfft)
c_fft = np.fft.irfft(A*A, nfft)
np.copyto(c_work, c_fft[:N])
np.maximum(c_work,0.0,out=c_work)
score = 2.0*n*float(np.max(c_work))
if not finite: a=ones? maybe.
best_score=score; best_a=a.copy(); stable? not needed.
```
Loop:
```
b1=0.9; b2=0.999; b1_t=1.0; b2_t=1.0; t=0
no=0; cur_pat=int(patience); t_start=time.monotonic(); last_print=1e100; restarts=0
while time.monotonic() < end_time:
   # compute c/A/score
   A = np.fft.rfft(a, nfft)
   c_fft = np.fft.irfft(A*A, nfft)
   np.copyto(c_work, c_fft[:N])
   np.maximum(c_work, 0.0, out=c_work)
   maxc = float(np.max(c_work))
   score = 2.0*n*maxc
   if not np.isfinite(score) or not np.isfinite(maxc):
       a[:] = best_a
       no += 10
       continue
   if score < best_score - 1e-12:
       best_score = score
       best_a[:] = a
       no = 0
       if best_score < last_print - 1e-7 or restarts==0:
           print(...)
           last_print = best_score
   else:
       no += 1
   if no >= cur_pat:
       # restart
       base_arr = best_a
       if rng.random() < 0.35:
           sym_arr = best_a + best_a[::-1]
           sym_arr *= 0.5
           sym_norm = normalize_positive(sym_arr)
           if sym_norm is not None:
               base_arr = sym_norm
       sigma = 0.35 * (1.0 - 0.7*progress?) Need progress compute before. Use progress at top.
```
Need progress for p and sigma. Compute after score update:
```

if progress <0: progress=0; if>1: progress=1
```
For restart sigma: `sigma = max(0.05, 0.4*(1.0 - 0.7*progress))`. Clip noise [-3,3].
```
       sigma = max(0.05, 0.45 * (1.0 - 0.65*progress))
       noise_work[:] = rng.normal(0.0, sigma, size=n)
       np.clip(noise_work, -3.0, 3.0, out=noise_work)
       np.exp(noise_work, out=tmp)
       a[:] = base_arr
       a *= tmp
       s = a.sum()
       if finite: a/=s; np.maximum(a,1e-300,out=a); a/=a.sum()
       else: a[:] = best_a
       m.fill(0); v.fill(0); b1_t=1; b2_t=1; t=0; no=0; cur_pat=max(40,cur_pat//2); restarts+=1
       continue
   # gradient
   p = p_start + (p_end - p_start)*progress
   if no > cur_pat//3:
       p = min(2000.0, p * 1.5)
   if p < 0.05: p=0.05
   A already current, c_work current
   fft_power_gradient(c_work, A, n, nfft, p, r_work, wrev_work, G_work)
   meanG = float(np.dot(a, G_work))
   np.subtract(G_work, meanG, out=h_work)
   h_work *= a
   scale = float(np.mean(np.abs(h_work)))
   if not finite(scale) or scale < 1e-30:
       no += max(1, cur_pat//5)
       continue
   np.divide(h_work, scale, out=stepg)
   t += 1
   b1_t *= b1; b2_t *= b2
   m *= b1; m += (1.0 - b1) * stepg
   v *= b2; v += (1.0 - b2) * (stepg * stepg)
   np.divide(m, 1.0 - b1_t, out=q_work)
   np.divide(v, 1.0 - b2_t, out=sqrtv)
   np.sqrt(sqrtv, out=sqrtv)
   sqrtv += 1e-8
   np.divide(q_work, sqrtv, out=delta)
   lr = init_lr * (0.5 + 0.5*(1.0 - progress))
   if no > 30:
       lr *= 0.8
   delta *= -lr
   np.clip(delta, -2.0, 2.0, out=delta)
   np.exp(delta, out=tmp)
   a *= tmp
   s = float(a.sum())
   if not finite or s <=1e-300:
      a[:] = best_a
      m.fill(0); v.fill(0); b1_t=1; b2_t=1; t=0
   else:
      a /= s
      np.maximum(a, 1e-300, out=a)
      a /= a.sum()
```

def gradient_sparse_into(a, A, c, n, nfft, p, k, direct_threshold, r_top, idx_top, w_top, wrev_work, G_work):
    N=2*n-1
    M=float(np.max(c))
    if M <= 1e-300:
       G_work.fill(0); G_work[0]=1; return
    if k <= 0: k=1
    if k > len(idx_top): k = len(idx_top)
    if k >= N:
       # fallback dense? But we don't have dense func here. Use all? idx length insufficient. Set k=N? cannot. Instead in optimize use dense if k>=N.
    if k == 1:
       idx_top[0] = int(np.argmax(c))
       w_top[0] = 1.0
    else:
       part = np.argpartition(c, -k)
       take = part[N-k:N]
       idx_top[:k] = take
       np.divide(c[idx_top[:k]], M, out=r_top[:k])
       np.clip(r_top[:k], 0.0, 1.0, out=r_top[:k])
       np.power(r_top[:k], p, out=r_top[:k])
       s = float(np.sum(r_top[:k]))
       if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
          w_top[:k] = 0.0
          # find max in top
          amax = int(np.argmax(r_top[:k])) # but r_top zero? use c?
          amax = int(np.argmax(c[idx_top[:k]]))
          w_top[amax] = 1.0
       else:
          np.divide(r_top[:k], s, out=w_top[:k])
    if k <= direct_threshold:
       G_work.fill(0.0)
       for ii in range(k):
          wi = float(w_top[ii])
          if wi <= 1e-16: continue
          kk = int(idx_top[ii])
          lo = kk - n + 1
          if lo < 0: lo=0
          hi = kk
          if hi >= n: hi = n-1
          if lo <= hi:
             G_work[lo:hi+1] += wi * a[lo:hi+1][::-1]
    else:
       wrev_work.fill(0.0)
       for ii in range(k):
           wi = float(w_top[ii])
           if wi <= 0.0: continue
           wrev_work[N - 1 - int(idx_top[ii])] = wi
       W = np.fft.rfft(wrev_work, nfft)
       h = np.fft.irfft(A * W, nfft)
       if n == 1: G_work[0]=h[0]
       else: np.copyto(G_work, h[N-1:n-2:-1])
       np.maximum(G_work,0.0,out=G_work)
    # ensure nonnegative for direct too
    if direct_threshold and k <= direct_threshold:
       np.maximum(G_work,0.0,out=G_work)
```
In direct, `G_work[...] += wi * a[...][::-1]`; result nonnegative. Good.
If k>direct, `h` new; G_work may have negative from round, clip.
For k>=N not handled; in optimize, if topk >= N: use dense perhaps. Since max_top 512 < N, no.

Dense gradient function similar but uses r_dense.
```
def gradient_dense_into(c, A, n,nfft,p,r_work,wrev_work,G_work):
   N=2*n-1; M=max; ... r_work length N
   wrev.fill(0); wrev[:N]=r_work[::-1]; W=rfft; h=irfft; copy G.
```
Use r_work length N.

In optimize, before gradient:
```
if p <= DENSE_P_THRESHOLD or topk >= N:
    gradient_dense_into(...)
else:
    gradient_sparse_into(...)
```
But if p low and N huge, dense okay. If p low but no_imp? Dense good.

```python
"""
Optimizer for sequences minimizing 2*n*max(a*a)/(sum a)^2.

Main ideas:
- Work on the simplex (sum a = 1), since the objective is scale invariant.
- Use log-space Adam-style updates on positive sequences.
- Use adaptive power-weighted gradients:
    w_k ~ (c_k / max(c))^p,
  where c = a * a.  For small p this is a broad flattening signal; for large p
  it focuses on the dominant convolution peaks.
- Low-p phases use dense weights over all convolution coefficients; high-p
  phases use top-k weights and either direct banded gradients or FFT-based
  gradients for the gradient vector.
- Multiple lengths and diverse starting shapes are explored, then the best
  candidates are refined, polished, and locally perturbed near the deadline.
"""

import numpy as np
import time

DENSE_P_THRESHOLD = 6.0
DIRECT_TOPK_THRESHOLD = 32
MAX_TOPK = 512


def normalize_positive(arr):
    """Clip to [0,1000], make finite, positive, and normalize sum to 1."""
    try:
        a = np.asarray(arr, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    except Exception:
        return None
    if a.size == 0:
        return None
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300:
        return None
    a = a / s
    a = np.maximum(a, 1e-300)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s > 0.0 and np.isfinite(s):
        a = a / s
    return a


def resample_sequence(parent, n):
    """Linearly resample a parent sequence to length n."""
    p = np.asarray(parent, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    m = int(p.size)
    if m == 0:
        return None
    if m == n:
        return p
    x_old = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, m)
    x_new = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)
    vals = np.interp(x_new, x_old, p)
    return np.maximum(vals, 0.0)


def smooth_random(n, rng):
    """Generate a smooth positive random shape."""
    z = rng.standard_normal(n)
    L = 1 << (max(1, 2 * n - 1) - 1).bit_length()
    F = np.fft.rfft(z, L)
    k = np.fft.rfftfreq(L) * L
    cutoff = max(1.0, n / 4.0)
    F = F * np.exp(-0.5 * (k / cutoff) ** 2)
    y = np.fft.irfft(F, L)[:n]
    return np.abs(y) + 0.1


def push_init(inits, name, arr):
    """Add a normalized candidate to a list of (name, array) pairs."""
    a = normalize_positive(arr)
    if a is not None:
        inits.append((name, a))


def make_inits(n, rng, parent):
    """Create diverse normalized starting sequences of length n."""
    inits = []
    i = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64)

    push_init(inits, "ones", np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64))

    # Coefficients of (1 - z)^(-1/2), truncated.  Good one-sided edge shape.
    alpha = np.empty(n, dtype=np.float64)
    alpha[0] = 1.0
    if n > 1:
        j = np.arange(1, n, dtype=np.float64)
        ratios = (2.0 * j - 1.0) / (2.0 * j)
        alpha[1:] = np.cumprod(ratios)
        push_init(inits, "binom", alpha)
        push_init(inits, "binom_rev", alpha[::-1])
        push_init(inits, "binom_mix", alpha + 0.25 * alpha[::-1])
        push_init(inits, "binom_mix2", 0.8 * alpha + 0.2 * alpha[::-1])

    # One-sided powers.
    for pval in (0.2, 0.3, 0.4, 0.5):
        push_init(inits, "pow_" + str(pval), (i + 1.0) ** (-pval))

    # Two-sided mild powers.
    push_init(inits, "twopow_5_2", (i + 1.0) ** (-0.5) + (n - i + 1.0) ** (-0.2))
    push_init(inits, "twopow_3_3", (i + 1.0) ** (-0.3) + (n - i + 1.0) ** (-0.3))
    push_init(inits, "twopow_4_1", (i + 1.0) ** (-0.4) + 0.25 * (n - i + 1.0) ** (-0.1))

    x = (i + 0.5) / float(n)
    for pval in (-0.25, 0.0, 0.25):
        vals = np.sin(np.pi * x) ** pval
        push_init(inits, "sin_" + str(pval), vals)

    push_init(inits, "cos1", 1.0 + 0.5 * np.cos(2.0 * np.pi * x))
    push_init(inits, "cos2", 1.0 + 0.3 * np.cos(4.0 * np.pi * x))
    push_init(inits, "ramp", 1.0 + 0.5 * x)

    g = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
    if n > 1:
        g[1::2] = 0.45
    push_init(inits, "gapped", g)

    push_init(inits, "rand", 0.1 + rng.random(n))
    push_init(inits, "smooth", smooth_random(n, rng))

    if parent is not None:
        ps = np.asarray(parent, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
        if ps.size == n:
            push_init(inits, "parent", ps)
        elif ps.size > 0:
            push_init(inits, "parent_rs", resample_sequence(ps, n))

    return inits


def initial_score(a):
    """Return (score, normalized_a) from a candidate array."""
    a = normalize_positive(a)
    if a is None:
        return np.inf, None
    n = int(a.size)
    N = 2 * n - 1
    nfft = 1 << (N - 1).bit_length()
    A = np.fft.rfft(a, nfft)
    c = np.fft.irfft(A * A, nfft)[:N]
    c = np.maximum(c, 0.0)
    return float(2.0 * n * np.max(c)), a


def select_candidates(cands, max_count):
    """Select diverse high-scoring-first candidates without lambdas."""
    if not cands or max_count <= 0:
        return []
    cands_sorted = sorted(cands)
    selected = []
    seen_ids = set()
    seen_ns = set()
    n_distinct_limit = min(max_count, len(cands_sorted))

    for cand in cands_sorted:
        if cand[2] not in seen_ns:
            selected.append(cand)
            seen_ids.add(cand[1])
            seen_ns.add(cand[2])
            if len(selected) >= n_distinct_limit:
                break

    for cand in cands_sorted:
        if cand[1] not in seen_ids:
            selected.append(cand)
            seen_ids.add(cand[1])
            if len(selected) >= max_count:
                break

    return selected


def select_refine(results, max_count):
    """Pick top refined candidates, diversifying by length."""
    if not results or max_count <= 0:
        return []
    results_sorted = sorted(results)
    selected = []
    seen_ids = set()
    seen_ns = set()

    for cand in results_sorted:
        if cand[2] not in seen_ns:
            selected.append(cand)
            seen_ids.add(cand[1])
            seen_ns.add(cand[2])
            if len(selected) >= max_count:
                break

    for cand in results_sorted:
        if cand[1] not in seen_ids:
            selected.append(cand)
            seen_ids.add(cand[1])
            if len(selected) >= max_count:
                break

    return selected


def gradient_dense_into(c, A, n, nfft, p, r_work, wrev_work, G_work):
    """Dense power-weighted gradient G_i = sum_k w_k * d c_k / d a_i (up to scale)."""
    N = 2 * n - 1
    M = float(np.max(c))
    if M <= 1e-300:
        G_work.fill(0.0)
        G_work[0] = 1.0
        return G_work

    np.divide(c, M, out=r_work)
    np.clip(r_work, 0.0, 1.0, out=r_work)
    np.power(r_work, p, out=r_work)

    s = float(np.sum(r_work))
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        r_work.fill(0.0)
        r_work[int(np.argmax(c))] = 1.0
        s = 1.0
    else:
        r_work /= s

    wrev_work.fill(0.0)
    wrev_work[:N] = r_work[::-1]

    W = np.fft.rfft(wrev_work, nfft)
    h = np.fft.irfft(A * W, nfft)

    if n == 1:
        G_work[0] = h[0]
    else:
        start = N - 1
        stop = n - 2
        np.copyto(G_work, h[start:stop:-1])

    np.maximum(G_work, 0.0, out=G_work)
    return G_work


def gradient_sparse_into(a, A, c, n, nfft, p, k, direct_threshold,
                         r_top, idx_top, w_top, wrev_work, G_work):
    """Top-k power-weighted gradient.  Uses direct banded sums for tiny k."""
    N = 2 * n - 1
    M = float(np.max(c))
    if M <= 1e-300:
        G_work.fill(0.0)
        G_work[0] = 1.0
        return G_work

    if k <= 0:
        k = 1
    if k > len(idx_top):
        k = int(len(idx_top))
    if k >= N:
        # Fallback to a very dense local behavior: just use the current max.
        k = 1

    if k == 1:
        idx_top[0] = int(np.argmax(c))
        w_top[0] = 1.0
    else:
        part = np.argpartition(c, -k)
        take = part[N - k:N]
        idx_top[:k] = take

        np.divide(c[idx_top[:k]], M, out=r_top[:k])
        np.clip(r_top[:k], 0.0, 1.0, out=r_top[:k])
        np.power(r_top[:k], p, out=r_top[:k])

        s = float(np.sum(r_top[:k]))
        if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
            w_top[:k] = 0.0
            amax = int(np.argmax(c[idx_top[:k]]))
            w_top[amax] = 1.0
        else:
            np.divide(r_top[:k], s, out=w_top[:k])

    if k <= direct_threshold:
        G_work.fill(0.0)
        for ii in range(k):
            wi = float(w_top[ii])
            if wi <= 1e-16:
                continue
            kk = int(idx_top[ii])
            lo = kk - n + 1
            if lo < 0:
                lo = 0
            hi = kk
            if hi >= n:
                hi = n - 1
            if lo <= hi:
                G_work[lo:hi + 1] += wi * a[lo:hi + 1][::-1]
        np.maximum(G_work, 0.0, out=G_work)
    else:
        wrev_work.fill(0.0)
        for ii in range(k):
            wi = float(w_top[ii])
            if wi <= 0.0:
                continue
            wrev_work[N - 1 - int(idx_top[ii])] = wi

        W = np.fft.rfft(wrev_work, nfft)
        h = np.fft.irfft(A * W, nfft)

        if n == 1:
            G_work[0] = h[0]
        else:
            start = N - 1
            stop = n - 2
            np.copyto(G_work, h[start:stop:-1])

        np.maximum(G_work, 0.0, out=G_work)

    return G_work


def optimize_log(a0, end_time, rng, init_lr, p_start, p_end, topk_start, topk_end, patience, label):
    """
    Local optimizer.

    Maintains a on the simplex and uses Adam-style updates in log space:
        a <- normalize(a * exp(delta_x))
    where delta_x follows Adam on the tangent gradient of a power-weighted
    convolution objective.
    """
    a = normalize_positive(a0)
    if a is None:
        try:
            n = int(np.asarray(a0).size)
        except Exception:
            n = 1024
        n = max(16, n)
        a = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) / float(n)

    n = int(a.size)
    N = 2 * n - 1
    nfft = 1 << (N - 1).bit_length()

    m = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    v = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)

    c_work = np.empty(N, dtype=np.float64)
    r_dense = np.empty(N, dtype=np.float64)
    r_top = np.empty(MAX_TOPK, dtype=np.float64)
    idx_top = np.empty(MAX_TOPK, dtype=np.int64)
    w_top = np.empty(MAX_TOPK, dtype=np.float64)
    wrev_work = np.empty(nfft, dtype=np.float64)
    G_work = np.empty(n, dtype=np.float64)
    h_work = np.empty(n, dtype=np.float64)
    stepg = np.empty(n, dtype=np.float64)
    q_work = np.empty(n, dtype=np.float64)
    sqrtv = np.empty(n, dtype=np.float64)
    delta = np.empty(n, dtype=np.float64)
    tmp = np.empty(n, dtype=np.float64)
    noise_work = np.empty(n, dtype=np.float64)

    A = np.fft.rfft(a, nfft)
    c_fft = np.fft.irfft(A * A, nfft)
    np.copyto(c_work, c_fft[:N])
    np.maximum(c_work, 0.0, out=c_work)

    score = float(2.0 * n * np.max(c_work))
    if not np.isfinite(score):
        a = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) / float(n)
        A = np.fft.rfft(a, nfft)
        c_fft = np.fft.irfft(A * A, nfft)
        np.copyto(c_work, c_fft[:N])
        np.maximum(c_work, 0.0, out=c_work)
        score = float(2.0 * n * np.max(c_work))

    best_score = score
    best_a = a.copy()

    b1 = 0.9
    b2 = 0.999
    b1_t = 1.0
    b2_t = 1.0
    t = 0

    no_imp = 0
    cur_pat = int(max(20, patience))
    t_start = time.monotonic()
    last_print_time = 0.0
    last_print_score = 1e100
    restarts = 0

    while time.monotonic() < end_time:
        A = np.fft.rfft(a, nfft)
        c_fft = np.fft.irfft(A * A, nfft)
        np.copyto(c_work, c_fft[:N])
        np.maximum(c_work, 0.0, out=c_work)

        maxc = float(np.max(c_work))
        score = float(2.0 * n * maxc)

        if not np.isfinite(score) or not np.isfinite(maxc):
            a[:] = best_a
            no_imp += max(10, cur_pat // 4)
            continue

        if score < best_score - 1e-12:
            best_score = score
            best_a[:] = a
            no_imp = 0
            now_time = time.monotonic()
            if best_score < last_print_score - 1e-8 or now_time - last_print_time > 8.0:
                print("%s: %f (elapsed %.1fs, p restarts=%d)" %
                      (label, best_score, now_time - t_start, restarts), flush=True)
                last_print_score = best_score
                last_print_time = now_time
        else:
            no_imp += 1

        dur = end_time - t_start
        if dur <= 1e-9:
            break
        progress = (time.monotonic() - t_start) / dur
        if progress < 0.0:
            progress = 0.0
        elif progress > 1.0:
            progress = 1.0

        if no_imp >= cur_pat:
            base_arr = best_a
            if rng.random() < 0.35:
                sym_arr = best_a + best_a[::-1]
                sym_arr *= 0.5
                sym_norm = normalize_positive(sym_arr)
                if sym_norm is not None:
                    base_arr = sym_norm

            sigma = max(0.05, 0.45 * (1.0 - 0.65 * progress))
            noise_work[:] = rng.normal(0.0, sigma, size=n)
            np.clip(noise_work, -3.0, 3.0, out=noise_work)
            np.exp(noise_work, out=tmp)

            a[:] = base_arr
            a *= tmp
            s = float(a.sum())
            if np.isfinite(s) and s > 1e-300:
                a /= s
                np.maximum(a, 1e-300, out=a)
                a /= a.sum()
            else:
                a[:] = best_a

            m.fill(0.0)
            v.fill(0.0)
            b1_t = 1.0
            b2_t = 1.0
            t = 0
            no_imp = 0
            cur_pat = max(30, cur_pat // 2)
            restarts += 1
            continue

        p = float(p_start + (p_end - p_start) * progress)
        if p < 0.05:
            p = 0.05
        if no_imp > cur_pat // 3:
            p = min(1000.0, p * 1.5)

        topk = int(topk_start + (topk_end - topk_start) * progress)
        if topk < 4:
            topk = 4
        if topk > MAX_TOPK:
            topk = MAX_TOPK
        if no_imp > cur_pat // 3:
            topk = min(MAX_TOPK, topk + 32)
        if topk > N:
            topk = N

        use_dense = (p <= DENSE_P_THRESHOLD) or (topk >= N) or (topk > MAX_TOPK)
        if use_dense:
            gradient_dense_into(c_work, A, n, nfft, p, r_dense, wrev_work, G_work)
        else:
            gradient_sparse_into(a, A, c_work, n, nfft, p, topk, DIRECT_TOPK_THRESHOLD,
                                 r_top, idx_top, w_top, wrev_work, G_work)

        meanG = float(np.dot(a, G_work))
        np.subtract(G_work, meanG, out=h_work)
        h_work *= a

        scale = float(np.mean(np.abs(h_work)))
        if not np.isfinite(scale) or scale <= 1e-30:
            no_imp += max(1, cur_pat // 5)
            continue

        np.divide(h_work, scale, out=stepg)

        t += 1
        b1_t *= b1
        b2_t *= b2

        m *= b1
        m += (1.0 - b1) * stepg

        v *= b2
        stepg2 = stepg * stepg
        v += (1.0 - b2) * stepg2

        np.divide(m, 1.0 - b1_t, out=q_work)
        np.divide(v, 1.0 - b2_t, out=sqrtv)
        np.sqrt(sqrtv, out=sqrtv)
        sqrtv += 1e-8

        np.divide(q_work, sqrtv, out=delta)

        lr = init_lr * (0.5 + 0.5 * (1.0 - progress))
        if no_imp > 30:
            lr *= 0.8
        delta *= -lr

        np.clip(delta, -2.0, 2.0, out=delta)
        np.exp(delta, out=tmp)
        a *= tmp

        s = float(a.sum())
        if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300:
            a[:] = best_a
            m.fill(0.0)
            v.fill(0.0)
            b1_t = 1.0
            b2_t = 1.0
            t = 0
        else:
            a /= s
            np.maximum(a, 1e-300, out=a)
            a /= a.sum()

    return best_score, best_a.copy()


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400.0, **kwargs):
    """Search for a strong non-negative sequence and return it as a list."""
    try:
        budget_s = float(budget_s)
    except Exception:
        budget_s = 2400.0
    budget_s = max(120.0, budget_s)

    rng = np.random.default_rng(int(seed) + 101)

    start = time.monotonic()
    deadline = start + budget_s - 30.0
    if deadline - start < 60.0:
        deadline = start + max(10.0, budget_s - 5.0)

    parent = None
    try:
        if "height_sequence_1" in globals():
            pp = globals().get("height_sequence_1", None)
            if pp is not None:
                parent = np.asarray(pp, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
                if parent.size == 0:
                    parent = None
    except Exception:
        parent = None

    if budget_s < 600:
        max_len = 4096
    elif budget_s < 1500:
        max_len = 8192
    else:
        max_len = 16384

    lengths = []
    seen_len = set()

    if parent is not None and 256 <= parent.size <= 20000:
        lengths.append(int(parent.size))
        seen_len.add(int(parent.size))

    for tlen in (4096, 8192, 12288, 16384):
        if 512 <= tlen <= max_len and tlen not in seen_len:
            lengths.append(tlen)
            seen_len.add(tlen)

    if not lengths:
        lengths = [min(4096, max_len)]

    # Build and score initial candidates.
    cands = []
    cid = 0
    for nlen in lengths:
        inits = make_inits(nlen, rng, parent)
        for name, arr in inits:
            sc, anorm = initial_score(arr)
            if np.isfinite(sc) and anorm is not None:
                cands.append((sc, cid, int(nlen), name, anorm))
                cid += 1

    if not cands:
        nlen = lengths[0]
        arr = np.ones(nlen, dtype=np.float64) / float(nlen)
        cands.append((2.0, 0, int(nlen), "fallback", arr))

    # Current best from initial candidates.
    best_score = np.inf
    best_a = None
    for cand in cands:
        if cand[0] < best_score:
            best_score = cand[0]
            best_a = cand[4].copy()

    if best_a is None:
        best_a = np.ones(1024, dtype=np.float64) / 1024.0
        best_score = 2.0

    print("best initial: %.6f, candidates=%d, lengths=%s" % (best_score, len(cands), lengths), flush=True)

    t0 = time.monotonic()

    # If very little time remains, just quick-refine the best initial.
    if deadline - t0 < 60.0:
        if time.monotonic() < deadline - 5.0:
            sc, aa = optimize_log(best_a, deadline - 5.0, rng,
                                  0.05, 10.0, 120.0, 16, 64, 50, "quick")
            if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
                best_score = sc
                best_a = aa
        out = []
        for val in best_a:
            val = float(val)
            if val < 0.0:
                val = 0.0
            elif val > 1000.0:
                val = 1000.0
            out.append(val)
        return out

    # Exploration of diverse promising initial candidates.
    explore_frac = 0.25 if parent is not None else 0.35
    explore_end = t0 + explore_frac * (deadline - t0)
    available = explore_end - t0
    max_explore = int(max(1, min(len(cands), available / 18.0)))
    selected = select_candidates(cands, max_explore)

    results = []
    rid = 0

    if selected and available > 10.0:
        current = t0
        per = max(8.0, available / float(max(1, len(selected))))
        for cand in selected:
            if time.monotonic() >= explore_end - 2.0:
                break
            t_end = current + per
            if t_end > explore_end:
                t_end = explore_end
            sc, aa = optimize_log(cand[4], t_end, rng,
                                  0.18, 0.3, 40.0, 8, 64, 80,
                                  "explore_" + str(cand[3]))
            if np.isfinite(sc):
                results.append((sc, rid, cand[2], "explore_" + str(cand[3]), aa))
                if sc < best_score:
                    best_score = sc
                    best_a = aa.copy()
            rid += 1
            current = time.monotonic()

    current = time.monotonic()
    remaining = deadline - current

    # Refinement of a few best refined candidates.
    refine_end = min(t0 + 0.60 * (deadline - t0), deadline - 120.0)
    if current < refine_end and results:
        top_results = select_refine(results, 3 if remaining > 900 else 2)
        per = max(20.0, (refine_end - current) / float(max(1, len(top_results))))
        for rr in top_results:
            if time.monotonic() >= refine_end - 2.0:
                break
            t_end = current + per
            if t_end > refine_end:
                t_end = refine_end
            sc, aa = optimize_log(rr[4], t_end, rng,
                                  0.05, 6.0, 200.0, 16, 128, 250,
                                  "refine_" + str(rr[3]))
            if np.isfinite(sc):
                results.append((sc, rid, rr[2], "refine_" + str(rr[3]) + "2", aa))
                if sc < best_score:
                    best_score = sc
                    best_a = aa.copy()
            rid += 1
            current = time.monotonic()

    current = time.monotonic()

    # Polish the global best.
    polish_end = min(t0 + 0.90 * (deadline - t0), deadline - 80.0)
    if best_a is not None and current < polish_end:
        sc, aa = optimize_log(best_a, polish_end, rng,
                              0.015, 40.0, 600.0, 32, 256, 600, "polish")
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
            best_score = sc
            best_a = aa.copy()

    current = time.monotonic()
    target = 1.5027

    # Small local perturbations near the end, only if still above target.
    if best_a is not None and current < deadline - 70 and best_score > target:
        left = deadline - 70 - current
        if left > 80.0:
            n_best = int(best_a.size)
            sigma_base = max(0.03, 0.10 * min(1.0, (best_score - 1.4) / 0.2))
            per = left / 3.0
            for mi in range(3):
                if time.monotonic() >= deadline - 40.0:
                    break
                t_end = current + per
                if t_end > deadline - 40.0:
                    t_end = deadline - 40.0

                base_arr = best_a
                if rng.random() < 0.5:
                    sym_arr = best_a + best_a[::-1]
                    sym_arr *= 0.5
                    sym_norm = normalize_positive(sym_arr)
                    if sym_norm is not None:
                        base_arr = sym_norm

                sigma = sigma_base * (1.0 + 0.35 * mi)
                noise = rng.normal(0.0, sigma, size=n_best)
                np.clip(noise, -2.5, 2.5, out=noise)
                mult = np.exp(noise)
                a_init = base_arr * mult
                a_init = normalize_positive(a_init)
                if a_init is None:
                    a_init = best_a.copy()

                sc, aa = optimize_log(a_init, t_end, rng,
                                      0.006, 160.0, 800.0, 64, 256, 120,
                                      "micro%d" % mi)
                if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
                    best_score = sc
                    best_a = aa.copy()
                current = time.monotonic()

    # Final short polish.
    if best_a is not None and time.monotonic() < deadline - 15.0:
        sc, aa = optimize_log(best_a, deadline - 15.0, rng,
                              0.005, 220.0, 900.0, 80, 256, 300, "final_polish")
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
            best_score = sc
            best_a = aa.copy()

    # Final sanity normalization.
    if best_a is None:
        best_a = np.ones(1024, dtype=np.float64) / 1024.0

    best_a = normalize_positive(best_a)
    if best_a is None:
        best_a = np.ones(1024, dtype=np.float64) / 1024.0

    out = []
    for val in best_a:
        val = float(val)
        if val < 0.0:
            val = 0.0
        elif val > 1000.0:
            val = 1000.0
        out.append(val)

    print("FINAL INTERNAL %.8f n=%d elapsed=%.1fs" %
          (best_score, int(best_a.size), time.monotonic() - start), flush=True)
    return out
```
